A MIDSUMMER [[Night|NIGHT]]’S DREAM
FRANCIS MERES mentions A Midsummer [[Night]]’s Dream in his Palladis Tamia, of 1598, and it was first printed in 1600. The Folio (1623) version offers significant variations apparently deriving from performance, and is followed in the present edition. It has often been thought that Shakespeare wrote the play for an aristocratic wedding, but there is no evidence to support this speculation, and the 1600 title-page states that it had been ’sundry times publicly acted’ by the Lord Chamberlain’s Men. In stylistic variation it resembles [[Love]]’s Labour’s Lost: both plays employ a wide variety of verse measures and rhyme schemes, along with prose that is sometimes (as in [[Bottom]]’s account of his dream, 4.1.202―15) rhetorically patterned. Probably it was written in 1594 or 1595, either just before or just after [[Romeo]] and [[Juliet]].
Shakespeare built his own plot from diverse elements of literature, drama, legend, and folklore, supplemented by his [[Imagination|imagination]] and observation. There are four main strands. One, which forms the basis of the action, shows the preparations for the [[Marriage|marriage]] of [[Theseus]], Duke of Athens, to [[Hippolyta]], [[Queen]] of the Amazons, and (in the last act) its celebration. This is indebted to Chaucer’s Knight’s Tale, as is the play’s second strand, the [[Love|love]] story of [[Lysander]] and [[Hermia]] (who elope to escape her father’s opposition) and of [[Demetrius]]. In Chaucer, two young men fall in [[Love|love]] with the same girl and quarrel over her; Shakespeare adds the comic complication of another girl ([[Helena]]) jilted by, but still loving, one of the young men. A third strand shows the efforts of a group of Athenian workmen—the ‘mechanicals’—led by [[Bottom]] the Weaver to prepare a play, Pyramus and Thisbe (based mainly on [[Arthur]] Golding’s [[Translation|translation]] of Ovid’s Metamorphoses) for performance at the Duke’s wedding. The mechanicals themselves belong rather to [[Elizabethan England]] than to [[Ancient Greece|ancient Greece]]. [[Bottom]]’s partial transformation into an ass has many literary precedents. Fourthly, Shakespeare depicts a quarrel between [[Oberon]] and [[Titania]], [[King]] and [[Queen]] of the Fairies. [[Oberon]]’s attendant, Robin Goodfellow, a [[Puck|puck]] (or pixie), interferes mischievously in the workmen’s rehearsals and the affairs of the [[lovers]]. The fairy part of the play owes something to both folklore and literature; Robin Goodfellow was a [[Well|well]]-known figure about whom Shakespeare could have read in Reginald Scot’s Discovery of Witchcraft (1586).
A Midsummer [[Night]]’s Dream offers a glorious celebration of the powers of the human [[Imagination|imagination]] while also making comic capital out of its limitations. It is one of Shakespeare’s most polished achievements, a poetic drama of exquisite grace, wit, and humanity. In performance, its imaginative [[unity]] has sometimes been violated, but it has become one of Shakespeare’s most popular plays, with a special appeal for the young.
THE PERSONS OF THE PLAY
[[Theseus|THESEUS]], Duke of Athens
[[Hippolyta|HIPPOLYTA]], [[Queen]] of the Amazons, betrothed to [[Theseus]]
PHILOSTRATE, Master of the Revels to [[Theseus]]
EGEUS, father of [[Hermia]]
[[Hermia|HERMIA]], daughter of Egeus, in [[Love|love]] with [[Lysander]]
[[Lysander|LYSANDER]], loved by [[Hermia]]
[[Demetrius|DEMETRIUS]], suitor to [[Hermia]]
[[Helena|HELENA]], in [[Love|love]] with [[Demetrius]]

[[Oberon|OBERON]], [[King]] of Fairies
[[Titania|TITANIA]], [[Queen]] of Fairies
ROBIN GOODFELLOW, a [[Puck|puck]]
Peter QUINCE, a carpenter
Nick [[Bottom|BOTTOM]], a weaver
Francis FLUTE, a bellows-mender
Tom SNOUT, a tinker
SNUG, a joiner
Robin STARVELING, a tailor

Attendant lords and fairies

A Midsummer [[Night]]’s Dream

### Atto 1, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Theseus]], [[Hippolyta]], and Philostrate, with others |
| [[Theseus\|THESEUS]] | Now, fair [[Hippolyta]], our nuptial hour<br>Draws on apace. Four happy days bring in<br>Another [[Moon\|moon]]—but O, methinks how slow<br>This old [[Moon\|moon]] wanes! She lingers my desires<br>Like to a stepdame or a dowager<br>Long withering out a young man’s revenue. |
| [[Hippolyta\|HIPPOLYTA]] | Four days will quickly steep themselves in [[Night\|night]],<br>Four nights will quickly dream away the [[Time\|time]];<br>And then the [[Moon\|moon]], like to a silver bow<br>New bent in heaven, shall behold the [[Night\|night]]<br>Of our solemnities. |
| [[Theseus\|THESEUS]] | Go, Philostrate,<br>Stir up the Athenian youth to merriments.<br>Awake the pert and nimble spirit of mirth.<br>Turn [[Melancholy\|melancholy]] forth to funerals—<br>The pale companion is not for our pomp. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Philostrate |
| [[Theseus\|THESEUS]] | [[Hippolyta]], I wooed thee with my [[Sword\|sword]],<br>And won thy [[Love\|love]] doing thee injuries.<br>But I will wed thee in another key—<br>With pomp, with triumph, and with revelling. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Egeus and his daughter [[Hermia]], and [[Lysander]] and [[Demetrius]] |
| EGEUS | Happy be [[Theseus]], our renowned Duke. |
| [[Theseus\|THESEUS]] | Thanks, good Egeus. What’s the news with thee? |
| EGEUS | Full of vexation come I, with complaint<br>Against my [[Child\|child]], my daughter [[Hermia]].—<br>Stand forth [[Demetrius]].—My noble lord,<br>This man hath my consent to marry her.—<br>Stand forth [[Lysander]].—And, my gracious Duke,<br>This hath bewitched the bosom of my [[Child\|child]].<br>Thou, thou, [[Lysander]], thou hast given her rhymes,<br>And interchanged [[Love\|love]] tokens with my [[Child\|child]].<br>Thou hast by moonlight at her [[Window\|window]] sung<br>With feigning voice verses of feigning [[Love\|love]],<br>And stol’n the impression of her fantasy<br>With bracelets of thy hair, rings, gauds, conceits,<br>Knacks, trifles, nosegays, sweetmeats—messengers<br>Of strong prevailment in unhardened youth. 35<br>With cunning hast thou filched my daughter’s heart,<br>Turned her obedience which is due to me<br>To stubborn harshness. And, my gracious Duke,<br>Be it so she will not here before your grace<br>Consent to marry with [[Demetrius]],<br>I beg the ancient privilege of Athens:<br>As she is mine, I may dispose of her,<br>Which shall be either to this gentleman<br>Or to her [[Death\|death]], according to our law<br>Immediately provided in that case. |
| [[Theseus\|THESEUS]] | What say you, [[Hermia]]? Be advised, fair maid.<br>To you your father should be as a god,<br>One that composed your beauties, yea, and one<br>To whom you are but as a form in wax,<br>By him imprinted, and within his power<br>To leave the figure or disfigure it.<br>[[Demetrius]] is a worthy gentleman. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | So is [[Lysander]]. |
| [[Theseus\|THESEUS]] | In himself he is,<br>But in this kind, wanting your father’s voice,<br>The other must be held the worthier. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | I would my father looked but with my eyes. |
| [[Theseus\|THESEUS]] | Rather your eyes must with his judgement look. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | I do entreat your grace to pardon me.<br>I know not by what power I am made bold,<br>Nor how it may concern my modesty<br>In such a presence here to plead my thoughts,<br>But I beseech your grace that I may know<br>The worst that may befall me in this case<br>If I refuse to wed [[Demetrius]]. |
| [[Theseus\|THESEUS]] | Either to die the [[Death\|death]], or to abjure<br>For ever the society of men.<br>Therefore, fair [[Hermia]], question your desires.<br>Know of your youth, examine [[Well\|well]] your [[Blood\|blood]],<br>Whether, if you yield not to your father’s choice,<br>You can endure the livery of a nun,<br>For aye to be in shady cloister mewed,<br>To live a barren sister all your life,<br>Chanting faint hymns to the cold fruitless [[Moon\|moon]].<br>Thrice blessed they that master so their [[Blood\|blood]]<br>To undergo such maiden pilgrimage;<br>But earthlier happy is the [[Rose\|rose]] distilled<br>Than that which, withering on the virgin thorn,<br>Grows, lives, and dies in single blessedness. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | So will I grow, so live, so die, my lord,<br>Ere I will yield my virgin patent up<br>Unto his lordship whose unwishèd yoke<br>My [[Soul\|soul]] consents not to give sovereignty. |
| [[Theseus\|THESEUS]] | Take [[Time\|time]] to pause, and by the next new [[Moon\|moon]]—<br>The sealing day betwixt my [[Love\|love]] and me<br>For everlasting bond of fetlowship—<br>Upon that day either prepare to die<br>For disobedience to your father’s will,<br>Or else to wed [[Demetrius]], as he would,<br>Or on [[Diana]]’s altar to protest<br>For aye austerity and single life. |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | Relent, sweet [[Hermia]]; and, [[Lysander]], yield<br>Thy crazed title to my certain right. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | You have her father’s [[Love\|love]], [[Demetrius]];<br>Let me have [[Hermia]]’s. Do you marry him. |
| EGEUS | Scornful [[Lysander]]! True, he hath my [[Love\|love]];<br>And what is mine my [[Love\|love]] shall render him,<br>And she is mine, and all my right of her<br>I do estate unto [[Demetrius]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] to [[Theseus]] |
| EGEUS | I am, my lord, as [[Well\|well]] derived as he,<br>As [[Well\|well]] possessed. My [[Love\|love]] is more than his,<br>My fortunes every way as fairly ranked,<br>If not with vantage, as [[Demetrius]];<br>And—which is more than all these boasts can be—<br>I am beloved of beauteous [[Hermia]].<br>Why should not I then prosecute my right?<br>[[Demetrius]]—I’ll avouch it to his head-<br>Made [[Love\|love]] to Nedar’s daughter, [[Helena]],<br>And won her [[Soul\|soul]], and she, sweet lady, dotes,<br>Devoutly dotes, dotes in idolatry<br>Upon this spotted and inconstant man. |
| [[Theseus\|THESEUS]] | I must confess that I have heard so much,<br>And with [[Demetrius]] thought to have spoke thereof;<br>But, being over-full of [[Self\|self]] affairs,<br>My mind did lose it. But, [[Demetrius]], come;<br>And come, Egeus. You shall go with me.<br>I have some private schooling for you both.<br>For you, fair [[Hermia]], look you arm yourself<br>To fit your fancies to your father’s will,<br>Or else the law of Athens yields you up—<br>Which by no means we may extenuate—<br>To [[Death\|death]] or to a vow of single life.<br>Come, my [[Hippolyta]]; what cheer, my [[Love\|love]]?—<br>[[Demetrius]] and Egeus, go along.<br>I must employ you in some business<br>Against our nuptial, and confer with you<br>Of something nearly that concerns yourselves. |
| EGEUS | With duty and [[Desire\|desire]] we follow you. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt all but [[Lysander]] and [[Hermia]] |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | How now, my [[Love\|love]]? Why is your cheek so pate ?<br>How chance the roses there do fade so fast? |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | Belike for want of rain, which I could [[Well\|well]]<br>Beteem them from the tempest of my eyes. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | Ay me, for aught that I could ever read,<br>Could ever hear by tale or [[history]],<br>The course of true [[Love\|love]] never did run smooth,<br>But either it was different in btood— |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | O [[Cross\|cross]]!—too high to be enthralled to low. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | Or else misgrafted in respect of years— |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | O spite!—too old to be engaged to young. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | Or merit stood upon the choice of friends— |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | O hell!—to choose [[Love\|love]] by another’s eyes. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | Or if there were a sympathy in choice,<br>War, [[Death\|death]], or sickness did lay siege to it,<br>Making it momentany as a sound,<br>Swift as a [[Shadow\|shadow]], short as any dream,<br>Brief as the [[Lightning\|lightning]] in the collied [[Night\|night]],<br>That, in a spleen, unfolds both heaven and earth,<br>And, ere a man hath power to say ‘Behold!’<br>The jaws of darkness do devour it up.<br>So quick bright things come to confusion. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | If then true [[lovers]] have been ever crossed,<br>It stands as an edict in destiny.<br>Then let us teach our trial patience,<br>Because it is a customary [[Cross\|cross]],<br>As due to [[Love\|love]] as thoughts, and [[Dreams\|dreams]], and sighs,<br>Wishes, and [[Tears\|tears]], poor fancy’s followers. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | A good [[Persuasion\|persuasion]]. Therefore hear me, [[Hermia]].<br>I have a widow aunt, a dowager<br>Of great revenue, and she hath no [[Child\|child]],<br>And she respects me as her only son.<br>From Athens is her [[house]] remote seven leagues.<br>There, gentle [[Hermia]], may I marry thee,<br>And to that place the sharp Athenian law<br>Cannot pursue us. If thou lov’st me then,<br>Steal forth thy father’s [[house]] tomorrow [[Night\|night]],<br>And in the wood, a league without the town,<br>Where I did meet thee once with [[Helena]]<br>To do observance to a morn of May,<br>There will I stay for thee. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | My good [[Lysander]],<br>I swear to thee by Cupid’s strongest bow,<br>By his best arrow with the golden head,<br>By the simplicity of [[Venus]]’ doves,<br>By that which knitteth souls and prospers loves,<br>And by that [[Fire\|fire]] which burned the Carthage [[Queen\|queen]]<br>When the false Trojan under sail was seen;<br>By all the vows that ever men have broke—<br>In number more than ever women spoke—<br>In that same place thou hast appointed me<br>Tomorrow truly will I meet with thee. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | Keep promise, [[Love\|love]]. Look, here comes [[Helena]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Helena]] |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | God [[Speed\|speed]], fair [[Helena]]. Whither away? |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | Call you me fair? That ’fair’ again unsay.<br>[[Demetrius]] loves your fair—O happy fair!<br>Your eyes are lodestars, and your tongue’s sweet air<br>More tuneable than lark to shepherd’s ear<br>When wheat is green, when hawthorn buds appear.<br>Sickness is catching. O, were favour so!<br>Your words I catch, fair [[Hermia]]; ere I go,<br>My ear should catch your voice, my eye your eye,<br>My tongue should catch your tongue’s sweet melody.<br>Were the world mine, [[Demetrius]] being bated,<br>The rest I’d give to be to you translated.<br>O, teach me how you look, and with what art<br>You sway the motion of [[Demetrius]]’ heart. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | I frown upon him, yet he loves me still. |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | O that your frowns would teach my smiles such skill! |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | I give him curses, yet he gives me [[Love\|love]]. |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | O that my prayers could such affection move! |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | The more I hate, the more he follows me. |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | The more I [[Love\|love]], the more he hateth me. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | His folly, [[Helen]], is no fault of mine. |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | None but your [[Beauty\|beauty]]; would that fault were mine! |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | Take comfort. He no more shall see my face.<br>[[Lysander]] and myself will fly this place.<br>Before the [[Time\|time]] I did [[Lysander]] see<br>Seemed Athens as a paradise to me.<br>O then, what graces in my [[Love\|love]] do dwell,<br>That he hath turned a heaven unto a hell? |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | [[Helen]], to you our minds we will unfold.<br>Tomorrow [[Night\|night]], when [[Phoebe]] doth behold<br>Her silver visage in the wat’ry glass,<br>Decking with liquid pearl the bladed grass—<br>A [[Time\|time]] that [[lovers]]’ sleights doth still conceal—<br>Through Athens’ gates have we devised to steal. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | And in the wood where often you and I<br>Upon faint primrose beds were wont to lie,<br>Emptying our bosoms of their counsel sweet,<br>There my [[Lysander]] and myself shall meet,<br>And thence from Athens turn away our eyes<br>To seek new friends and stranger companies.<br>Farewell, sweet playfellow. Pray thou for us,<br>And good luck grant thee thy [[Demetrius]].—<br>Keep word, [[Lysander]]. We must starve our sight<br>From [[lovers]]’ food till morrow deep midnight. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | I will, my [[Hermia]]. Exit [[Hermia]]<br>[[Helena]], adieu.<br>As you on him, [[Demetrius]] dote on you. Exit |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | How happy some o’er other some can be!<br>Through Athens I am thought as fair as she.<br>But what of that? [[Demetrius]] thinks not so.<br>He will not know what all but he do know.<br>And as he errs, doting on [[Hermia]]’s eyes,<br>So I, admiring of his qualities.<br>Things base and vile, holding no quantity,<br>[[Love]] can transpose to form and dignity.<br>[[Love]] looks not with the eyes, but with the mind,<br>And therefore is winged Cupid painted blind.<br>Nor hath [[Love\|love]]’s mind of any judgement taste;<br>Wings and no eyes figure unheedy haste.<br>And therefore is [[Love\|love]] said to be a [[Child\|child]]<br>Because in choice he is so oft beguiled.<br>As waggish boys in game themselves forswear,<br>So the boy [[Love]] is perjured everywhere.<br>For ere [[Demetrius]] looked on [[Hermia]]’s eyne<br>He hailed down oaths that he was only mine,<br>And when this hail some heat from [[Hermia]] felt,<br>So he dissolved, and showers of oaths did melt.<br>I will go tell him of fair [[Hermia]]’s flight.<br>Then to the wood will he tomorrow [[Night\|night]]<br>Pursue her, and for this intelligence<br>If I have thanks it is a dear expense.<br>But herein mean I to enrich my [[Pain\|pain]],<br>To have his sight thither and back again. Exit |

### Atto 1, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Quince the carpenter, and Snug the joiner, and [[Bottom]] the weaver, and Flute the bellows-mender, and Snout the tinker, and Starveling the tailor |
| QUINCE | Is all our company here? |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | You were best to call them generally, man by man, according to the scrip. |
| QUINCE | Here is the scroll of every man’s name which is thought fit through all Athens to play in our interlude before the Duke and the Duchess on his wedding day at [[Night\|night]]. |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | First, good Peter Quince, say what the play treats on; then read the names of the actors; and so grow to a point. 10 |
| QUINCE | Marry, our play is The Most Lamentable [[Comedy]] and Most Cruel [[Death]] of Pyramus and Thisbe. |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | A very good piece of work, I assure you, and a merry. Now, good Peter Quince, call forth your actors by the scroll. Masters, spread yourselves. |
| QUINCE | Answer as I call you. Nick [[Bottom]], the weaver? |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | Ready. Name what part I am for, and proceed. |
| QUINCE | You, Nick [[Bottom]], are set down for Pyramus. |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | What is Pyramus? A [[Lover\|lover]] or a [[Tyrant\|tyrant]]? |
| QUINCE | A [[Lover\|lover]], that kills himself most gallant for [[Love\|love]]. |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | That will ask some [[Tears\|tears]] in the true performing of it. If I do it, let the audience look to their eyes. I will move stones. I will condole, in some measure. To the rest.—Yet my chief [[Humour\|humour]] is for a [[Tyrant\|tyrant]]. I could play ’erc’les rarely, or a part to tear a cat in, to make all split.<br>The raging rocks<br>And shivering shocks<br>Shall break the locks<br>Of [[Prison\|prison]] gates,<br>And Phibus’ car<br>Shall shine from far<br>And make and mar<br>The foolish Fates.<br>This was lofty. Now name the rest of the players.—This is ’erc’les’ vein, a [[Tyrant\|tyrant]]’s vein. A [[Lover\|lover]] is more condoling. |
| QUINCE | Francis Flute, the bellows-mender? |
| FLUTE | Here, Peter Quince. |
| QUINCE | Flute, you must take Thisbe on you. |
| FLUTE | What is Thisbe? A wand’ring knight? |
| QUINCE | It is the lady that Pyramus must [[Love\|love]]. |
| FLUTE | Nay, [[Faith\|faith]], let not me play a woman. I have a beard coming. |
| QUINCE | That’s all one. You shall play it in a mask, and you may speak as small as you will. |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | An I may hide my face, let me play Thisbe too.<br>I’ll speak in a monstrous little voice: ‘Thisne, Thisne!’—<br><br>‘Ah Pyramus, my [[Lover\|lover]] dear, thy Thisbe dear and lady<br>dear.’ |
| QUINCE | No, no, you must play Pyramus; and Flute, you Thisbe. |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | [[Well]], proceed. |
| QUINCE | Robin Starveling, the tailor? |
| STARVELING | Here, Peter Quince. |
| QUINCE | Robin Starveling, you must play Thisbe’s mother.<br>Tom Snout, the tinker? |
| SNOUT | Here, Peter Quince. |
| QUINCE | You, Pyramus’ father; myself, Thisbe’s father.<br>Snug the joiner, you the [[Lion\|lion]]’s part; and I [[Hope\|hope]] here is a play fitted. |
| SNUG | Have you the [[Lion\|lion]]’s part written? Pray you, if it be, give it me; for I am slow of study. |
| QUINCE | You may do it extempore, for it is nothing but roaring. |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | Let me play the [[Lion\|lion]] too. I will roar that I will do any man’s heart good to hear me. I will roar that I will make the Duke say ‘Let him roar again; let him roar again’. |
| QUINCE | An you should do it too terribly you would fright the Duchess and the ladies that they would shriek, and that were enough to hang us all. |
| ALL THE REST | That would hang us, every mother’s son. |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | I grant you, friends, if you should fright the ladies out of their wits they would have no more discretion but to hang us, but I will aggravate my voice so that I will roar you as gently as any sucking dove. I will roar you an ’twere any [[Nightingale\|nightingale]]. |
| QUINCE | You can play no part but Pyramus; for Pyramus is a sweet-faced man; a proper man as one shall see in a [[Summer\|summer]]’s day; a most lovely, gentlemanlike man. Therefore you must needs play Pyramus. |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | [[Well]], I will undertake it. What beard were I best to play it in? |
| QUINCE | Why, what you will. |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | I will discharge it in either your straw-colour beard, your orange-tawny beard, your purple-in-grain beard, or your French-[[Crown\|crown]]-colour beard, your perfect yellow. |
| QUINCE | Some of your French crowns have no hair at all, and then you will play bare faced. But masters, here are your parts, and I am to entreat you, request you, and [[Desire\|desire]] you to con them by tomorrow [[Night\|night]], and meet me in the palace wood a mile without the town by moonlight. There will we rehearse; for if we meet in the [[City\|city]] we shall be dogged with company, and our devices known. In the meantime I will draw a bill of properties such as our play wants. I pray you fail me not. |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | We will meet, and there we may rehearse most obscenely and courageously. Take pains; be perfect. Adieu. |
| QUINCE | At the Duke’s oak we meet. |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | Enough. Hold, or cut bowstrings. Exeunt |

### Atto 2, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Fairy at one door and Robin Goodfellow, a [[Puck\|puck]], at another |
| ROBIN | How now, spirit, whither wander you? |
| FAIRY | Over [[Hill\|hill]], over dale,<br>Thorough bush, thorough brier,<br>Over park, over pale,<br>Thorough flood, thorough [[Fire\|fire]]:<br>I do wander everywhere<br>Swifter than the moonës sphere,<br>And I serve the Fairy [[Queen]]<br>To dew her orbs upon the green.<br>The cowslips tall her pensioners be.<br>In their gold coats spots you see;<br>Those be rubies, fairy favours;<br>In those freckles live their savours.<br>I must go seek some dewdrops here,<br>And hang a pearl in every cowslip’s ear.<br>Farewell, thou lob of spirits; I’ll be gone.<br>Our [[Queen]] and all her elves come here anon. |
| ROBIN | The [[King]] doth keep his revels here tonight.<br>Take heed the [[Queen]] come not within his sight,<br>For [[Oberon]] is passing fell and wroth 20<br>Because that she, as her attendant, hath<br>A lovely boy stol’n from an Indian [[King\|king]].<br>She never had so sweet a changeling;<br>And jealous [[Oberon]] would have the [[Child\|child]]<br>Knight of his train, to trace the forests wild.<br>But she perforce withholds the loved boy,<br>Crowns him with [[Flowers\|flowers]], and makes him all her joy.<br>And now they never meet in grove, or green,<br>By fountain clear, or spangled starlight sheen,<br>But they do square, that all their elves for [[Fear\|fear]]<br>Creep into acorn cups, and hide them there. |
| FAIRY | Either I mistake your shape and making quite<br>Or else you are that shrewd and knavish sprite<br>Called Robin Goodfellow. Are not you he<br>That frights the maidens of the villag‘ry,<br>Skim milk, and sometimes labour in the quern,<br>And bootless make the breathless housewife churn,<br>And sometime make the drink to bear no barm—<br>Mislead [[Night\|night]] wanderers, laughing at their harm?<br>Those that ‘hobgoblin’ call you, and ‘sweet [[Puck\|puck]]’,<br>You do their work, and they shall have good luck.<br>Are not you he? |
| ROBIN | Thou speak’st aright;<br>I am that merry wanderer of the [[Night\|night]].<br>I jest to [[Oberon]], and make him smile<br>When I a fat and bean-fed [[Horse\|horse]] beguile,<br>Neighing in likeness of a filly foal;<br>And sometime lurk I in a gossip’s bowl<br>In very likeness of a roasted crab,<br>And when she drinks, against her lips I bob,<br>And on her withered dewlap pour the ale.<br>The wisest aunt telling the saddest tale<br>Sometime for three-foot stool mistaketh me;<br>Then slip I from her bum. Down topples she,<br>And ’tailor’ cries, and falls into a cough,<br>And then the whole choir hold their hips, and laugh,<br>And waxen in their mirth, and sneeze, and swear<br>A merrier hour was never wasted there.— |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Oberon]] the [[King]] of Fairies at one door, with his train, and [[Titania]] the [[Queen]] at another, with hers |
| ROBIN | But make room, fairy: here comes [[Oberon]]. |
| FAIRY | And here my mistress. Would that he were gone. |
| [[Oberon\|OBERON]] | I’ll met by moonlight, proud [[Titania]]. |
| [[Titania\|TITANIA]] | What, jealous [[Oberon]]?—Fairies, skip hence.<br>I have forsworn his bed and company. |
| [[Oberon\|OBERON]] | Tarry, rash wanton. Am not I thy lord? |
| [[Titania\|TITANIA]] | Then I must be thy lady; but I know<br>When thou hast stol’n away from fairyland<br>And in the shape of Corin sat all day,<br>Playing on pipes of corn, and versing [[Love\|love]]<br>To amorous Phillida. Why art thou here<br>Come from the farthest step of India,<br>But that, forsooth, the bouncing Amazon,<br>Your buskined mistress and your warrior [[Love\|love]],<br>To [[Theseus]] must be wedded, and you come<br>To give their bed joy and prosperity? |
| [[Oberon\|OBERON]] | How canst thou thus for shame, [[Titania]],<br>Glance at my credit with [[Hippolyta]],<br>Knowing I know thy [[Love\|love]] to [[Theseus]]?<br>Didst not thou lead him through the glimmering [[Night\|night]]<br>From Perigouna whom he ravished,<br>And make him with fair Aegles break his [[Faith\|faith]],<br>With Ariadne and Antiopa? |
| [[Titania\|TITANIA]] | These are the forgeries of [[Jealousy\|jealousy]],<br>And never since the middle [[Summer\|summer]]’s [[Spring\|spring]]<br>Met we on [[Hill\|hill]], in dale, forest, or mead,<br>By paved fountain or by rushy brook,<br>Or in the beached margin of the sea<br>To [[Dance\|dance]] our ringlets to the whistling [[Wind\|wind]],<br>But with thy brawls thou hast disturbed our sport.<br>Therefore the winds, piping to us in vain,<br>As in [[Revenge\|revenge]] have sucked up from the sea<br>Contagious fogs which, falling in the land,<br>Hath every pelting [[River\|river]] made so proud<br>That they have overborne their continents.<br>The ox hath therefore stretched his yoke in vain,<br>The ploughman lost his sweat, and the green corn<br>Hath rotted ere his youth attained a beard.<br>The fold stands empty in the drowned field,<br>And crows are fatted with the murrain flock.<br>The nine men’s morris is filled up with mud,<br>And the quaint mazes in the wanton green<br>For lack of tread are undistinguishable.<br>The human mortals want their [[Winter\|winter]] cheer.<br>No [[Night\|night]] is now with [[Hymn\|hymn]] or [[carol]] blessed.<br>Therefore the [[Moon\|moon]], the governess of floods,<br>Pale in her anger washes all the air,<br>That rheumatic diseases do abound;<br>And thorough this distemperature we see<br>The [[Seasons\|seasons]] alter: hoary-headed frosts<br>Fall in the fresh lap of the crimson [[Rose\|rose]],<br>And on old Hiems’ thin and icy [[Crown\|crown]]<br>An odorous chaplet of sweet [[Summer\|summer]] buds<br>Is, as in mock’ry, set. The [[Spring\|spring]], the [[Summer\|summer]],<br>The childing [[Autumn\|autumn]], angry [[Winter\|winter]] change<br>Their wonted liveries, and the mazèd world<br>By their increase now knows not which is which;<br>And this same progeny of evils comes<br>From our [[debate]], from our dissension.<br>We are their parents and original. |
| [[Oberon\|OBERON]] | Do you amend it, then. It lies in you.<br>Why should [[Titania]] [[Cross\|cross]] her [[Oberon]]?<br>I do but beg a little changeling boy<br>To be my henchman. |
| [[Titania\|TITANIA]] | Set your heart at rest.<br>The fairyland buys not the [[Child\|child]] of me.<br>His mother was a vot‘ress of my order,<br>And in the spiced Indian air by [[Night\|night]]<br>Full often hath she gossiped by my side,<br>And sat with me on Neptune’s yellow sands,<br>Marking th’embarkèd traders on the flood,<br>When we have laughed to see the sails conceive<br>And grow big-bellied with the wanton [[Wind\|wind]],<br>Which she with pretty and with swimming gait<br>Following, her womb then rich with my young squire,<br>Would imitate, and sail upon the land<br>To fetch me trifles, and return again<br>As from a voyage, rich with merchandise.<br>But she, being mortal, of that boy did die;<br>And for her sake do I rear up her boy;<br>And for her sake I will not part with him. |
| [[Oberon\|OBERON]] | How long within this wood intend you stay? |
| [[Titania\|TITANIA]] | Perchance till after [[Theseus]]’ wedding day.<br>If you will patiently [[Dance\|dance]] in our round,<br>And see our moonlight revels, go with us.<br>If not, shun me, and I will spare your haunts. |
| [[Oberon\|OBERON]] | Give me that boy and I will go with thee. |
| [[Titania\|TITANIA]] | Not for thy fairy kingdom.—Fairies, away.<br>We shall chide downright if I longer stay. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[Titania]] and her train |
| [[Oberon\|OBERON]] | [[Well]], go thy way. Thou shalt not from this grove<br>Till I torment thee for this injury.—<br>My gentle [[Puck\|puck]], come hither. Thou rememb’rest<br>Since once I sat upon a promontory<br>And heard a [[Mermaid\|mermaid]] on a dolphin’s back<br>Uttering such dulcet and harmonious breath<br>That the rude sea grew civil at her [[Song\|song]]<br>And certain [[Stars\|stars]] shot madly from their spheres<br>To hear the sea-maid’s music? |
| ROBIN | I remember. |
| [[Oberon\|OBERON]] | That very [[Time\|time]] I saw, but thou couldst not,<br>Flying between the cold [[Moon\|moon]] and the earth<br>Cupid, all armed. A certain aim he took<br>At a fair vestal thronèd by the west,<br>And loosed his [[Love\|love]]-shaft smartly from his bow<br>As it should pierce a hundred thousand hearts.<br>But I might see young Cupid’s fiery shaft<br>Quenched in the chaste beams of the wat‘ry [[Moon\|moon]],<br>And the imperial vot’ress passed on,<br>In maiden meditation, fancy-free.<br>Yet marked I where the bolt of Cupid fell. 165<br>It fell upon a little western flower—<br>Before, milk-white; now, purple with [[Love\|love]]’s wound—<br>And maidens call it [[Love\|love]]-in-idleness.<br>Fetch me that flower; the herb I showed thee once.<br>The juice of it on sleeping eyelids laid<br>Will make or man or woman madly dote<br>Upon the next live creature that it sees.<br>Fetch me this herb, and be thou here again<br>Ere the leviathan can swim a league. |
| ROBIN | I’ll put a girdle round about the earth<br>In forty minutes. Exit |
| [[Oberon\|OBERON]] | Having once this juice<br>I’ll watch [[Titania]] when she is asleep,<br>And drop the liquor of it in her eyes.<br>The next thing then she waking looks upon—<br>Be it on [[Lion\|lion]], bear, or wolf, or bull,<br>On meddling monkey, or on busy ape—<br>She shall pursue it with the [[Soul\|soul]] of [[Love\|love]].<br>And ere I take this charm from off her sight—<br>As I can take it with another herb—<br>I’ll make her render up her page to me.<br>But who comes here? I am invisible,<br>And I will overhear their conference. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Demetrius]], [[Helena]] following him |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | I [[Love\|love]] thee not, therefore pursue me not.<br>Where is [[Lysander]], and fair [[Hermia]]?<br>The one I’ll slay, the other slayeth me.<br>Thou told’st me they were stol’n unto this wood,<br>And here am I, and wood within this wood<br>Because I cannot meet my [[Hermia]].<br>Hence, get thee gone, and follow me no more. |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | You draw me, you hard-hearted adamant,<br>But yet you draw not iron; for my heart<br>Is true as steel. Leave you your power to draw,<br>And I shall have no power to follow you. |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | Do I entice you? Do I speak you fair?<br>Or rather do I not in plainest [[Truth\|truth]]<br>Tell you I do not nor I cannot [[Love\|love]] you? |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | And even for that do I [[Love\|love]] you the more.<br>I am your spaniel, and, [[Demetrius]],<br>The more you beat me I will fawn on you.<br>Use me but as your spaniel: spurn me, strike me,<br>Neglect me, lose me; only give me leave,<br>Unworthy as I am, to follow you.<br>What worser place can I beg in your [[Love\|love]]—<br>And yet a place of high respect with me—<br>Than to be used as you use your dog? 210 |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | Tempt not too much the hatred of my spirit;<br>For I am sick when I do look on thee. |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | And I am sick when I look not on you. |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | You do impeach your modesty too much,<br>To leave the [[City\|city]] and commit yourself 215<br>Into the hands of one that loves you not;<br>To trust the opportunity of [[Night\|night]],<br>And the ill counsel of a [[Desert\|desert]] place,<br>With the rich worth of your virginity. |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | Your virtue is my privilege, for that 220<br>It is not [[Night\|night]] when I do see your face;<br>Therefore I think I am not in the [[Night\|night]],<br>Nor doth this wood lack worlds of company;<br>For you in my respect are all the world.<br>Then how can it be said I am [[Alone\|alone]], 225<br>When all the world is here to look on me? |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | I’ll run from thee, and hide me in the brakes,<br>And leave thee to the [[Mercy\|mercy]] of wild beasts. |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | The wildest hath not such a heart as you.<br>Run when you will. The story shall be changed: 230<br>[[Apollo]] flies, and Daphne holds the chase.<br>The dove pursues the griffin, the mild hind<br>Makes [[Speed\|speed]] to catch the tiger: bootless [[Speed\|speed]],<br>When cowardice pursues, and valour flies. |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | I will not stay thy questions. Let me go; 235<br>Or if thou follow me, do not believe<br>But I shall do thee mischief in the wood. |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | Ay, in the temple, in the town, the field,<br>You do me mischief. Fie, [[Demetrius]],<br>Your wrongs do set a scandal on my sex. 240<br>We cannot fight for [[Love\|love]] as men may do;<br>We should be wooed, and were not made to woo.<br>I’ll follow thee, and make a heaven of hell,<br>To die upon the hand I [[Love\|love]] so [[Well\|well]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Demetrius]], [[Helena]] following him |
| [[Oberon\|OBERON]] | Fare thee [[Well\|well]], nymph. Ere he do leave this grove<br>Thou shalt fly him, and he shall seek thy [[Love\|love]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Robin Goodfellow the [[Puck\|puck]] |
| [[Oberon\|OBERON]] | Hast thou the flower there? Welcome, wanderer. |
| ROBIN | Ay, there it is. |
| [[Oberon\|OBERON]] | I pray thee give it me.<br>I know a bank where the wild thyme blows,<br>Where oxlips and the nodding violet grows, 250<br>Quite overcanopied with luscious woodbine,<br>With sweet musk-roses, and with eglantine.<br>There sleeps [[Titania]] sometime of the [[Night\|night]],<br>Lulled in these [[Flowers\|flowers]] with dances and delight;<br>And there the snake throws her enamelled skin,<br>Weed wide enough to wrap a fairy in;<br>And with the juice of this I’ll streak her eyes,<br>And make her full of hateful fantasies.<br>Take thou some of it, and seek through this grove.<br>A sweet Athenian lady is in [[Love\|love]] 260<br>With a disdainful youth. Anoint his eyes;<br>But do it when the next thing he espies<br>May be the lady. Thou shalt know the man<br>By the Athenian garments he hath on.<br>Effect it with some care, that he may prove<br>More fond on her than she upon her [[Love\|love]];<br>And look thou meet me ere the first cock crow. |
| ROBIN | [[Fear]] not, my lord. Your servant shall do so. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt severally |

### Atto 2, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Titania]], [[Queen]] of Fairies, with her train |
| [[Titania\|TITANIA]] | Come, now a roundel and a fairy [[Song\|song]],<br>Then for the third part of a minute hence:<br>Some to kill cankers in the musk-[[Rose\|rose]] buds,<br>Some war with reremice for their leathern wings<br>To make my small elves coats, and some keep back<br>The clamorous owl, that nightly hoots and wonders<br>At our quaint spirits. Sing me now asleep;<br>Then to your offices, and let me rest.<br>She lies down. Fairies sing |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRST FAIRY |
| [[Titania\|TITANIA]] | You spotted snakes with [[Double\|double]] tongue,<br>Thorny hedgehogs, be not seen; 10<br>Newts and blindworms, do no wrong;<br>Come not near our Fairy [[Queen]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Chorus\|CHORUS]] dancing |
| [[Titania\|TITANIA]] | Philomel with melody,<br>Sing in our sweet lullaby;<br>Lulla, lulla, lullaby; lulla, lulla, lullaby. 15<br>Never harm<br>Nor spell nor charm<br>Come our lovely lady nigh.<br>So good [[Night\|night]], with lullaby. |
| FIRST FAIRY | Weaving spiders, come not here;<br>Hence, you long-legged spinners, hence;<br>Beetles black, approach not near;<br>Worm nor snail do no offence. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Chorus\|CHORUS]] dancing |
| FIRST FAIRY | Philomel with melody,<br>Sing in our sweet lullaby;<br>Lulla, lulla, lullaby; lulla, lulla, lullaby.<br>Never harm<br>Nor spell nor charm<br>Come our lovely lady nigh.<br>So good [[Night\|night]], with lullaby.<br>[[Titania]] sleeps |
| SECOND FAIRY | Hence, away. Now all is [[Well\|well]].<br>One aloof stand sentinel. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt all but [[Titania]] and the sentinel Enter [[Oberon]]. He drops the juice on [[Titania]]’s eyelids |
| [[Oberon\|OBERON]] | What thou seest when thou dost wake,<br>Do it for thy true [[Love\|love]] take;<br>[[Love]] and languish for his sake.<br>Be it ounce, or cat, or bear,<br>Pard, or boar with bristled hair,<br>In thy eye that shall appear<br>When thou wak’st, it is thy dear.<br>Wake when some vile thing is near. Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Lysander]] and [[Hermia]] |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | Fair [[Love\|love]], you faint with wand’ring in the wood,<br>And, to speak [[Truth\|truth]], I have forgot our way.<br>We’ll rest us, [[Hermia]], if you think it good,<br>And tarry for the comfort of the day. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | Be it so, [[Lysander]]. Find you out a bed;<br>For I upon this bank will rest my head. |
| *(didascalia)* | She lies down |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | One turf shall serve as pillow for us both;<br>One heart, one bed; two bosoms, and one troth. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | Nay, good [[Lysander]]; for my sake, my dear,<br>Lie further off yet; do not lie so near. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | O, take the sense, sweet, of my [[innocence]]!<br>[[Love]] takes the meaning in [[Love\|love]]’s conference—<br>I mean that my heart unto yours is knit,<br>So that but one heart we can make of it.<br>Two bosoms interchainèd with an oath;<br>So, then, two bosoms and a single troth.<br>Then by your side no bed-room me deny;<br>For lying so, [[Hermia]], I do not lie. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | [[Lysander]] riddles very prettily.<br>Now much beshrew my manners and my [[Pride\|pride]]<br>If [[Hermia]] meant to say [[Lysander]] lied.<br>But, gentle friend, for [[Love\|love]] and courtesy,<br>Lie further off, in humane modesty.<br>Such separation as may [[Well\|well]] be said<br>Becomes a virtuous bachelor and a maid,<br>So far be distant; and good [[Night\|night]], sweet friend.<br>Thy [[Love\|love]] ne’er alter till thy sweet life end. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | Amen, amen, to that fair [[prayer]] say I;<br>And then end life when I end loyalty.<br>Here is my bed; [[Sleep\|sleep]] give thee all his rest.<br>He lies down |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | With half that wish the wisher’s eyes be pressed. |
| *(didascalia)* | They [[Sleep\|sleep]] apart. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Robin Goodfellow the [[Puck\|puck]] |
| ROBIN | Through the forest have I gone,<br>But Athenian found I none<br>On whose eyes I might approve<br>This flower’s force in stirring [[Love\|love]].<br>[[Night]] and [[Silence\|silence]]. Who is here?<br>Weeds of Athens he doth wear.<br>This is he my master said<br>Despised the Athenian maid—<br>And here the maiden, sleeping sound<br>On the dank and dirty ground.<br>Pretty [[Soul\|soul]], she durst not lie<br>Near this lack-[[Love\|love]], this kill-courtesy.<br>Churl, upon thy eyes I throw<br>All the power this charm doth owe.<br>He drops the juice on [[Lysander]]’s eyelids<br><br>When thou wak’st, let [[Love\|love]] forbid<br>[[Sleep]] his seat on thy eyelid.<br>So, awake when I am gone.<br>For I must now to [[Oberon]]. Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Demetrius]] and [[Helena]], running |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | Stay, though thou kill me, sweet [[Demetrius]]. |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | I charge thee hence, and do not haunt me thus. |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | O, wilt thou darkling leave me? Do not so. |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | Stay, on thy peril; I [[Alone\|alone]] will go. Exit |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | O, I am out of breath in this fond chase.<br>The more my [[prayer]], the lesser is my grace.<br>Happy is [[Hermia]], wheresoe‘er she lies;<br>For she hath blessed and attractive eyes.<br>How came her eyes so bright? Not with salt [[Tears\|tears]]—<br>If so, my eyes are oft’ner washed than hers.<br>No, no; I am as ugly as a bear, 100<br>For beasts that meet me run away for [[Fear\|fear]].<br>Therefore no marvel though [[Demetrius]]<br>Do, as a [[Monster\|monster]], fly my presence thus.<br>What wicked and dissembling glass of mine<br>Made me compare with [[Hermia]]’s sphery eyne!<br>But who is here? [[Lysander]], on the ground?<br>Dead, or asleep? I see no [[Blood\|blood]], no wound.<br>[[Lysander]], if you live, good sir, awake. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | (awaking)<br>And run through [[Fire\|fire]] I will for thy sweet sake.<br>Transparent [[Helena]], [[Nature\|nature]] shows art<br>That through thy bosom makes me see thy heart.<br>Where is [[Demetrius]]? O, how fit a word<br>Is that vile name to perish on my [[Sword\|sword]]! |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | Do not say so, [[Lysander]]; say not so.<br>What though he [[Love\|love]] your [[Hermia]]? Lord, what<br>though?<br>Yet [[Hermia]] still loves you; then be content. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | Content with [[Hermia]]? No, I do repent<br>The tedious minutes I with her have spent.<br>Not [[Hermia]] but [[Helena]] I [[Love\|love]].<br>Who will not change a raven for a dove?<br>The will of man is by his reason swayed,<br>And reason says you are the worthier maid.<br>Things growing are not ripe until their season,<br>So I, being young, till now ripe not to reason.<br>And, touching now the point of human skill,<br>Reason becomes the marshal to my will,<br>And leads me to your eyes, where I o’erlook<br>[[Love]]’s stories written in [[Love\|love]]’s richest book. |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | Wherefore was I to this keen mockery born?<br>When at your hands did I deserve this scorn?<br>Is’t not enough, is’t not enough, young man,<br>That I did never—no, nor never can—<br>Deserve a sweet look from [[Demetrius]]’ eye,<br>But you must flout my insufficiency?<br>Good troth, you do me wrong; good sooth, you do,<br>In such disdainful manner me to woo.<br>But fare you [[Well\|well]]. Perforce I must confess<br>I thought you lord of more true gentleness.<br>O, that a lady of one man refused<br>Should of another therefore be abused! Exit |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | She sees not [[Hermia]]. [[Hermia]], [[Sleep\|sleep]] thou there,<br>And never mayst thou come [[Lysander]] near;<br>For as a surfeit of the sweetest things<br>The deepest loathing to the stomach brings,<br>Or as the heresies that men do leave<br>Are hated most of those they did deceive,<br>So thou, my surfeit and my heresy,<br>Of all be hated, but the most of me;<br>And all my powers, address your [[Love\|love]] and might<br>To [[Honour\|honour]] [[Helen]], and to be her knight. Exit |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | (awaking)<br>Help me, [[Lysander]], help me! Do thy best<br>To pluck this crawling serpent from my breast!<br>Ay me, for pity. What [[A Dream\|a dream]] was here?<br>[[Lysander]], look how I do quake with [[Fear\|fear]].<br>Methought a serpent ate my heart away,<br>And you sat smiling at his cruel prey.<br>[[Lysander]]—what, removed? [[Lysander]], lord—<br>What, out of hearing, gone? No sound, no word?<br>Alack, where are you? Speak an if you hear,<br>Speak, of all loves. I swoon almost with [[Fear\|fear]].<br>No? Then I [[Well\|well]] perceive you are not nigh.<br>Either [[Death\|death]] or you I’ll find immediately. Exit |

### Atto 3, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the clowns: Quince, Snug, [[Bottom]], Flute, Snout, and Starveling |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | Are we all met? |
| QUINCE | Pat, pat; and here’s a marvellous convenient place for our rehearsal. This green plot shall be our stage, this hawthorn brake our tiring-[[house]], and we will do it in action as we will do it before the Duke.<br>[[Bottom\|bottom]] Peter Quince? |
| QUINCE | What sayst thou, bully [[Bottom]]? |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | There are things in this [[Comedy\|comedy]] of Pyramus and Thisbe that will never please. First, Pyramus must draw a [[Sword\|sword]] to kill himself, which the ladies cannot abide. How answer you that? |
| SNOUT | By’r la’kin, a parlous [[Fear\|fear]]. |
| STARVELING | I believe we must leave the killing out, when all is done. |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | Not a whit. I have a device to make all [[Well\|well]]. Write me a prologue, and let the prologue seem to say we will do no harm with our swords, and that Pyramus is not killed indeed; and for the more better assurance, tell them that I, Pyramus, am not Pyramus, but [[Bottom]] the weaver. This will put them out of [[Fear\|fear]]. |
| QUINCE | [[Well]], we will have such a prologue; and it shall be written in eight and six. |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | No, make it two more: let it be written in eight and eight. |
| SNOUT | Will not the ladies be afeard of the [[Lion\|lion]]? |
| STARVELING | I [[Fear\|fear]] it, I promise you. |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | Masters, you ought to consider with yourself, to bring in—God shield us—a [[Lion\|lion]] among ladies is a most dreadful thing; for there is not a more fearful wild fowl than your [[Lion\|lion]] living, and we ought to look to’t. |
| SNOUT | Therefore another prologue must tell he is not a [[Lion\|lion]]. |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | Nay, you must name his name, and half his face must be seen through the [[Lion\|lion]]’s neck, and he himself must speak through, saying thus or to the same defect: ‘ladies’, or ‘fair ladies, I would wish you’ or ‘I would request you’ or ‘I would entreat you not to [[Fear\|fear]], not to tremble. My life for yours. If you think I come hither as a [[Lion\|lion]], it were pity of my life. No, I am no such thing. I am a man, as other men are’—and there, indeed, let him name his name, and tell them plainly he is Snug the joiner. |
| QUINCE | [[Well]], it shall be so; but there is two hard things: that is, to bring the moonlight into a chamber—for you know Pyramus and Thisbe meet by moonlight. |
| *(didascalia)* | SNOUT Doth the [[Moon\|moon]] shine that [[Night\|night]] we play our play? |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | A calendar, a catendar—took in the almanac, find out moonshine, find out moonshine. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Robin Goodfellow the [[Puck\|puck]], invisible |
| *(didascalia)* | QUINCE with a book Yes, it doth shine that [[Night\|night]]. |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | Why, then may you leave a casement of the great chamber [[Window\|window]] where we play open, and the [[Moon\|moon]] may shine in at the casement. |
| QUINCE | Ay, or else one must come in with a bush of thorns and a lantern and say he comes to disfigure, or to present, the person of Moonshine. Then there is another thing: we must have a wall in the great chamber; for Pyramus and Thisbe, says the story, did talk through the chink of a wall. |
| SNOUT | You can never bring in a wall. What say you, [[Bottom]]? |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | Some man or other must present Wall; and let him have some plaster, or some loam, or some roughcast about him, to signify ‘wall’; and let him hold his fingers thus, and through that cranny shall Pyramus and Thisbe whisper. |
| QUINCE | If that may be, then all is [[Well\|well]]. Come, sit down every mother’s son, and rehearse your parts. Pyramus, you begin. When you have spoken your speech, enter into that brake; and so everyone according to his cue. |
| ROBIN | (aside)<br>What hempen homespuns have we swagg’ring here<br>So near the cradle of the Fairy [[Queen]]?<br>What, a play toward? I’ll be an auditor—<br>An actor, too, perhaps, if I see cause. |
| QUINCE | Speak, Pyramus. Thisbe, stand forth. |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | (as Pyramus)<br>Thisbe, the [[Flowers\|flowers]] of odious savours sweet. |
| QUINCE | Odours, odours. |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | (as Pyramus) Odours savours sweet.<br>So hath thy breath, my dearest Thisbe dear.<br>But hark, a voice. Stay thou but here a while,<br>And by and by I will to thee appear. Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | ROBIN (aside) |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | A stranger Pyramus than e’er played here. Exit |
| FLUTE | Must I speak now ? |
| QUINCE | Ay, marry must you. For you must understand he goes but to see a noise that he heard, and is to come again. 86 |
| FLUTE | (as Thisbe)<br>Most radiant Pyramus, most lily-white of hue,<br>Of colour like the red [[Rose\|rose]] on triumphant brier;<br>Most bristly juvenile, and eke most lovely Jew,<br>As true as truest [[Horse\|horse]] that yet would never tire:<br>I’ll meet thee, Pyramus, at Ninny’s tomb. |
| QUINCE | Ninus’ tomb, man!—Why, you must not speak that yet. That you answer to Pyramus. You speak all your part at once, cues and all.—Pyramus, enter: your cue is past; it is ‘never tire’. |
| FLUTE O. | (As Thisbe) As true as truest [[Horse\|horse]] that yet would<br>never tire. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Robin leading [[Bottom]] with the ass-head |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | (as Pyramus)<br>If I were fair, Thisbe, I were only thine. |
| *(didascalia)* | QUINCE O monstrousl O strange! We are haunted. Pray, masters; fly, masters: help! The clowns all exeunt |
| ROBIN | I’ll follow you, I’ll lead you about a round,<br>Through bog, through bush, through brake,<br>through brier.<br>Sometime a [[Horse\|horse]] I’ll be, sometime a hound,<br>A hog, a headless bear, sometime a [[Fire\|fire]],<br>And neigh, and bark, and grunt, and roar, and burn,<br>Like [[Horse\|horse]], hound, hog, bear, [[Fire\|fire]], at every turn. Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Bottom]] again, with the ass-head |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | Why do they run away? This is a knavery of them to make me afeard. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Snout |
| SNOUT | O [[Bottom]], thou art changed. What do I see on thee? |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] What do you see? You see an ass-head of your own, do you? Exit Snout |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Quince |
| QUINCE | Bless thee, [[Bottom]], bless thee. Thou art translated. Exit |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | I see their knavery. This is to make an ass of me, to fright me, if they could; but I will not stir from this place, do what they can. I will walk up and down here, and I will sing, that they shall hear I am not afraid.<br>(Sings)<br>The ousel cock so black of hue,<br>With orange-tawny bill;<br>The throstle with his note so true,<br>The wren with little quill. |
| [[Titania\|TITANIA]] | (awaking)<br>What angel wakes me from my flow’ry bed? |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | (sings)<br>The finch, the sparrow, and the lark,<br>The plainsong cuckoo grey,<br>Whose note full many a man doth mark,<br>And dares not answer ‘Nay’—<br>for indeed, who would set his wit to so foolish a bird?<br>Who would give a bird the lie, though he cry ’Cuckoo’<br>never so? |
| [[Titania\|TITANIA]] | I pray thee, gentle mortal, sing again.<br>Mine ear is much enamoured of thy note;<br>So is mine eye enthralled to thy shape;<br>And thy fair virtue’s force perforce doth move me<br>On the first view to say, to swear, I [[Love\|love]] thee. |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | Methinks, mistress, you should have little reason for that. And yet, to say the [[Truth\|truth]], reason and [[Love\|love]] keep little company together nowadays—the more the pity that some honest neighbours will not make them friends. Nay, I can gleek upon occasion. |
| [[Titania\|TITANIA]] | Thou art as wise as thou art beautiful. |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | Not so, neither; but if I had wit enough to get out of this wood, I have enough to serve mine own turn. |
| [[Titania\|TITANIA]] | Out of this wood do not [[Desire\|desire]] to go.<br>Thou shalt remain here, whether thou wilt or no.<br>I am a spirit of no common rate:<br>The [[Summer\|summer]] still doth tend upon my state;<br>And I do [[Love\|love]] thee. Therefore go with me.<br>I’ll give thee fairies to attend on thee,<br>And they shall fetch thee jewels from the deep,<br>And sing while thou on pressed [[Flowers\|flowers]] dost [[Sleep\|sleep]];<br>And I will purge thy mortal grossness so<br>That thou shalt like an airy spirit go.<br>Peaseblossom, Cobweb, Mote, and Mustardseed! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter four fairies: Peaseblossom, Cobweb, Mote, and Mustardseed |
| [[Titania\|TITANIA]] | A FAIRY<br>Ready. |
| ANOTHER | And I. |
| ANOTHER | And I. |
| ANOTHER | And I. |
| *(didascalia)* | ALL FOUR Where shall we go? |
| [[Titania\|TITANIA]] | Be kind and courteous to this gentleman.<br>Hop in his walks, and gambol in his eyes.<br>Feed him with apricots and dewberries,<br>With purple grapes, green figs, and mulberries;<br>The honeybags steal from the humble-[[Bees\|bees]],<br>And for [[Night\|night]] tapers crop their waxen thighs<br>And light them at the fiery glow-worms’ eyes<br>To have my [[Love\|love]] to bed, and to arise;<br>And pluck the wings from painted butterflies<br>To fan the moonbeams from his sleeping eyes.<br>Nod to him, elves, and do him courtesies.<br>A FAIRY Hail, mortal. |
| *(didascalia)* | FANOTHER Hall. |
| ANOTHER | Hail. |
| ANOTHER | Hail. |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | I cry your worships [[Mercy\|mercy]], heartily.—I beseech your worship’s name. |
| COBWEB | Cobweb. |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | I shall [[Desire\|desire]] you of more acquaintance, good Master Cobweb. If I cut my finger, I shall make bold with you.—Your name, honest gentleman? |
| PEASEBLOSSOM | Peaseblossom.<br>[[Bottom\|bottom]] I pray you commend me to Mistress Squash, your mother, and to Master Peascod, your father. Good Master Peaseblossom, I shall [[Desire\|desire]] you of more acquaintance, too.—Your name, I beseech you, sir? |
| MUSTARDSEED | Mustardseed. |
| [[Bottom\|BOTTOM]] | Good Master Mustardseed, I know your patience [[Well\|well]]. That same cowardly giantlike ox-beef hath devoured many a gentleman of your [[house]]. I promise you your kindred hath made my eyes [[Water\|water]] ere now. I [[Desire\|desire]] you of more acquaintance, good Master Mustardseed. |
| [[Titania\|TITANIA]] | (to the Fairies)<br>Come, wait upon him, lead him to my bower.<br>The [[Moon\|moon]], methinks, looks with a wat’ry eye,<br>And when she weeps, weeps every little flower,<br>Lamenting some enforced chastity.<br>Tie up my [[Love\|love]]’s tongue; bring him silently. Exeunt |

### Atto 3, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Oberon]], [[King]] of Fairies |
| [[Oberon\|OBERON]] | I [[Wonder\|wonder]] if [[Titania]] be awaked,<br>Then what it was that next came in her eye,<br>Which she must dote on in extremity. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Robin Goodfellow |
| [[Oberon\|OBERON]] | Here comes my messenger. How now, mad spirit?<br>What nightrule now about this haunted grove? |
| ROBIN | My mistress with a [[Monster\|monster]] is in [[Love\|love]].<br>Near to her close and consecrated bower<br>While she was in her dull and sleeping hour<br>A crew of patches, rude mechanicals<br>That work for bread upon Athenian stalls,<br>Were met together to rehearse a play<br>Intended for great [[Theseus]]’ nuptial day.<br>The shallowest thickskin of that barren sort,<br>Who Pyramus presented, in their sport<br>Forsook his scene and entered in a brake,<br>When I did him at this advantage take.<br>An ass’s nole I fixèd on his head.<br>Anon his Thisbe must be answered,<br>And forth my mimic comes. When they him spy—<br>As wild geese that the creeping fowler eye,<br>Or russet-pated choughs, many in sort,<br>Rising and cawing at the gun’s report,<br>Sever themselves and madly sweep the sky—<br>So, at his sight, away his fellows fly,<br>And at our stamp here o’er and o’er one falls.<br>He ’Murder’ cries, and help from Athens calls.<br>Their sense thus weak, lost with their fears thus<br>strong,<br>Made senseless things begin to do them wrong.<br>For briers and thorns at their apparel snatch;<br>Some sleeves, some hats—from yielders all things catch.<br>I led them on in this distracted [[Fear\|fear]],<br>And left sweet Pyramus translated there;<br>When in that moment, so it came to pass,<br>[[Titania]] waked and straightway loved an ass. |
| [[Oberon\|OBERON]] | This falls out better than I could devise.<br>But hast thou yet latched the Athenian’s eyes<br>With the [[Love\|love]] juice, as I did bid thee do? |
| ROBIN | I took him sleeping; that is finished, too;<br>And the Athenian woman by his side,<br>That when he waked of force she must be eyed. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Demetrius]] and [[Hermia]] |
| [[Oberon\|OBERON]] | Stand close. This is the same Athenian. |
| ROBIN | This is the woman, but not this the man. |
| *(didascalia)* | They stand apart |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | O, why rebuke you him that loves you so?<br>Lay breath so bitter on your bitter foe. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | Now I but chide, but I should use thee worse;<br>For thou, I [[Fear\|fear]], hast given me cause to curse.<br>If thou hast slain [[Lysander]] in his [[Sleep\|sleep]],<br>Being o‘er shoes in [[Blood\|blood]], plunge in the deep,<br>And kill me too.<br>The sun was not so true unto the day<br>As he to me. Would he have stolen away<br>From sleeping [[Hermia]]? I’ll believe as soon<br>This whole earth may be bored, and that the [[Moon\|moon]]<br>May through the centre creep, and so displease<br>Her brother’s noontide with th’Antipodes.<br>It cannot be but thou hast murdered him.<br>So should a murderer look—so dead, so grim. |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | So should the murdered look, and so should I,<br>Pierced through the heart with your stern cruelty.<br>Yet you, the murderer, look as bright, as clear<br>As yonder [[Venus]] in her glimmering sphere. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | What’s this to my [[Lysander]]? Where is he?<br>Ah, good [[Demetrius]], wilt thou give him me? |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | I had rather give his carcass to my hounds. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | Out, dog; out, cur. Thou driv’st me past the bounds<br>Of maiden’s patience. Hast thou slain him then?<br>Henceforth be never numbered among men.<br>O, once tell true; tell true, even for my sake.<br>Durst thou have looked upon him being awake,<br>And hast thou killed him sleeping? O brave touch!<br><br>Could not a worm, an adder do so much?—<br>An adder did it, for with doubler tongue<br>Than thine, thou serpent, never adder stung. |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | You spend your passion on a misprised mood.<br>I am not guilty of [[Lysander]]’s [[Blood\|blood]],<br>Nor is he dead, for aught that I can tell. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | I pray thee, tell me then that he is [[Well\|well]]. |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | And if I could, what should I get therefor? |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | A privilege never to see me more;<br>And from thy hated presence part I so.<br>See me no more, whether he be dead or no. Exit |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | There is no following her in this fierce vein.<br>Here therefore for a while I will remain.<br>So sorrow’s heaviness doth heavier grow<br>For debt that bankrupt [[Sleep\|sleep]] doth sorrow owe,<br>Which now in some slight measure it will pay,<br>If for his tender here I make some stay.<br>He lies down and sleeps |
| [[Oberon\|OBERON]] | (to Robin)<br>What hast thou done? Thou hast mistaken quite,<br>And laid the [[Love\|love]] juice on some true [[Love\|love]]’s sight.<br>Of thy misprision must perforce ensue<br>Some true [[Love\|love]] turned, and not a false turned true. |
| ROBIN | Then fate o’errules, that, one man holding troth,<br>A million fail, confounding oath on oath. |
| [[Oberon\|OBERON]] | About the wood go swifter than the [[Wind\|wind]],<br>And [[Helena]] of Athens look thou find.<br>All fancy-sick she is, and pale of cheer<br>With sighs of [[Love\|love]] that costs the fresh [[Blood\|blood]] dear.<br>By some illusion see thou bring her here.<br>I’ll charm his eyes against she do appear. |
| ROBIN | I go, I go—look how I go,<br>Swifter than arrow from the Tartar’s bow. Exit |
| [[Oberon\|OBERON]] | Flower of this purple dye,<br>Hit with Cupid’s archery,<br>Sink in apple of his eye.<br>He drops the juice on [[Demetrius]]’ eyelids<br>When his [[Love\|love]] he doth espy,<br>Let her shine as gloriously<br>As the [[Venus]] of the sky.<br>When thou wak’st, if she be by,<br>Beg of her for remedy. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Robin Goodfellow, the [[Puck\|puck]] |
| ROBIN | Captain of our fairy band,<br>[[Helena]] is here at hand,<br>And the youth mistook by me,<br>Pleading for a [[Lover\|lover]]’s fee.<br>Shall we their fond pageant see?<br>Lord, what fools these mortals be! |
| [[Oberon\|OBERON]] | Stand aside. The noise they make<br>Will cause [[Demetrius]] to awake. |
| ROBIN | Then will two at once woo one.<br>That must needs be sport [[Alone\|alone]];<br>And those things do best please me<br>That befall prepost’rously. |
| *(didascalia)* | They stand apart. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Helena]], [[Lysander]] following her |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | Why should you think that I should woo in scorn?<br>Scorn and derision never come in [[Tears\|tears]].<br>Look when I vow, I weep; and vows so born,<br>In their nativity all [[Truth\|truth]] appears.<br>How can these things in me seem scorn to you,<br>Bearing the badge of [[Faith\|faith]] to prove them true? |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | You do advance your cunning more and more,<br>When [[Truth\|truth]] kills [[Truth\|truth]]—O devilish holy fray!<br>These vows are [[Hermia]]’s. Will you give her o’er?<br>Weigh oath with oath, and you will nothing weigh.<br>Your vows to her and me put in two scales<br>Will even weigh, and both as light as tales. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | I had no judgement when to her I swore. |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | Nor none, in my mind, now you give her o’er. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | [[Demetrius]] loves her, and he loves not you. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Helena\|HELENA]] |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | (awaking)<br>O [[Helen]], goddess, nymph, perfect, divine!<br>To what, my [[Love\|love]], shall I compare thine eyne?<br>Crystal is muddy. O, how ripe in show<br>Thy lips, those kissing cherries, tempting grow!<br>That pure congealed white—high Taurus’ [[Snow\|snow]],<br>Fanned with the eastern [[Wind\|wind]]—turns to a crow<br>When thou hold’st up thy hand. O, let me kiss<br>This princess of pure white, this seal of bliss! |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | O spite! O hell! I see you all are bent<br>To set against me for your merriment.<br>If you were civil, and knew courtesy,<br>You would not do me thus much injury.<br>Can you not hate me—as I know you do—<br>But you must join in souls to mock me too?<br>If you were men, as men you are in show,<br>You would not use a gentle lady so,<br>To vow and swear and superpraise my parts<br>When I am sure you hate me with your hearts.<br>You both are rivals and [[Love\|love]] [[Hermia]],<br>And now both rivals to mock [[Helena]].<br>A trim exploit, a manly enterprise—<br>To conjure [[Tears\|tears]] up in a poor maid’s eyes<br>With your derision. None of noble sort<br>Would so offend a virgin, and extort<br>A poor [[Soul\|soul]]’s patience, all to make you sport. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | You are unkind, [[Demetrius]]. Be not so.<br>For you [[Love\|love]] [[Hermia]]; this you know I know.<br>And here with all good will, with all my heart,<br>In [[Hermia]]’s [[Love\|love]] I yield you up my part;<br>And yours of [[Helena]] to me bequeath,<br>Whom I do [[Love\|love]], and will do till my [[Death\|death]]. |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | Never did mockers waste more idle breath. |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | [[Lysander]], keep thy [[Hermia]]. I will none.<br>If e’er I loved her, all that [[Love\|love]] is gone.<br>My heart to her but as guestwise sojourned<br>And now [[To Helen\|to Helen]] is it [[Home\|home]] returned,<br>There to remain. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | [[Helen]], it is not so. |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | Disparage not the [[Faith\|faith]] thou dost not know,<br>Lest to thy peril thou aby it dear. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Hermia]] |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | Look where thy [[Love\|love]] comes; yonder is thy dear. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | Dark [[Night\|night]], that from the eye his function takes,<br>The ear more quick of apprehension makes.<br>Wherein it doth impair the seeing sense,<br>It pays the hearing [[Double\|double]] recompense.<br>Thou art not by mine eye, [[Lysander]], found;<br>Mine ear, I thank it, brought me to thy sound.<br>But why unkindly didst thou leave me so? |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | Why should he stay whom [[Love\|love]] doth press to go? |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | What [[Love\|love]] could press [[Lysander]] from my side? |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | [[Lysander]]’s [[Love\|love]], that would not let him bide:<br>Fair [[Helena]], who more engilds the [[Night\|night]]<br>Than all yon fiery O’s and eyes of light.<br>Why seek’st thou me? Could not this make thee know<br>The hate I bare thee made me leave thee so? |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | You speak not as you think. It cannot be. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Helena\|HELENA]] side |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | Lo, she is one of this confederacy.<br>Now I perceive they have conjoined all three<br>To fashion this false sport in spite of me.—<br>Injurious [[Hermia]], most ungrateful maid,<br>Have you conspired, have you with these contrived<br>To bait me with this foul derision?<br>Is all the counsel that we two have shared—<br>The sisters’ vows, the hours that we have spent<br>When we have chid the hasty-footed [[Time\|time]]<br>For parting us—O, is all quite forgot?<br>All schooldays’ [[Friendship\|friendship]], [[Childhood\|childhood]] [[innocence]]?<br>We, [[Hermia]], like two artificial gods<br>Have with our needles created both one flower,<br>Both on one sampler, sitting on one cushion,<br>Both warbling of one [[Song\|song]], both in one key,<br>As if our hands, our sides, voices, and minds<br>Had been incorporate. So we grew together,<br>Like to a [[Double\|double]] cherry: seeming parted,<br>But yet an [[Union\|union]] in partition,<br>Two lovely berries moulded on one stem.<br>So, with two seeming bodies but one heart,<br>Two of the first—like coats in heraldry,<br>Due but to one and crowned with one crest.<br>And will you rend our ancient [[Love\|love]] asunder,<br>To join with men in scorning your poor friend?<br>It is not friendly, ’tis not maidenly.<br>Our sex as [[Well\|well]] as I may chide you for it,<br>Though I [[Alone\|alone]] do feel the injury. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | I am amazed at your passionate words.<br>I scorn you not. It seems that you scorn me. |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | Have you not set [[Lysander]], as in scorn,<br>To follow me, and praise my eyes and face?<br>And made your other [[Love\|love]], [[Demetrius]]—<br>Who even but now did spurn me with his foot—<br>To call me goddess, nymph, divine, and rare,<br>Precious, celestial? Wherefore speaks he this<br>To her he hates? And wherefore doth [[Lysander]]<br>Deny your [[Love\|love]] so rich within his [[Soul\|soul]],<br>And tender me, forsooth, affection,<br>But by your setting on, by your consent?<br>What though I be not so in grace as you,<br>So hung upon with [[Love\|love]], so fortunate,<br>But miserable most, to [[Love\|love]] unloved—<br>This you should pity rather than despise. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | I understand not what you mean by this. |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | Ay, do. Persever, counterfeit sad looks,<br>Make mouths upon me when I turn my back,<br>Wink each at other, hold the sweet jest up.<br>This sport [[Well\|well]] carried shall be chronicled.<br>If you have any pity, grace, or manners,<br>You would not make me such an argument.<br>But fare ye [[Well\|well]]. ’Tis partly my own fault,<br>Which [[Death\|death]] or absence soon shall remedy. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | Stay, gentle [[Helena]], hear my excuse,<br>My [[Love\|love]], my life, my [[Soul\|soul]], fair [[Helena]]. |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | O excellent! |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | (to [[Lysander]]) Sweet, do not scorn her so. |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | (to [[Lysander]])<br>If she cannot entreat I can compel. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | Thou canst compel no more than she entreat.<br>Thy threats have no more strength than her weak<br>prayers.—<br>[[Helen]], I [[Love\|love]] thee; by my life I do.<br>I swear by that which I will lose for thee<br>To prove him false that says I [[Love\|love]] thee not. |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | (to [[Helena]])<br>I say I [[Love\|love]] thee more than he can do. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | If thou say so, withdraw, and prove it too. |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | Quick, come. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | [[Lysander]], whereto tends all this? |
| *(didascalia)* | She takes him by the arm |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | Away, you Ethiope. |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | No, no, sir, yield.<br>Seem to break loose, take on as you would follow,<br>But yet come not. You are a tame man; go. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | (to [[Hermia]])<br>Hang off, thou cat, thou burr; vile thing, let loose,<br>Or I will shake thee from me like a serpent. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | Why are you grown so rude? What change is this,<br>Sweet [[Love\|love]]? |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | Thy [[Love\|love]]? Out, tawny Tartar, out; Out, loathed med’cine; O hated potion, hence. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | Do you not jest? |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | Yes, sooth, and so do you. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | [[Demetrius]], I will keep my word with thee. |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | I would I had your bond, for I perceive<br>A weak bond holds you. I’ll not trust your word. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | What, should I hurt her, strike her, kill her dead?<br>Although I hate her, I’ll not harm her so. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | What, can you do me greater harm than hate?<br>Hate me—wherefore O me, what news, my [[Love\|love]]?<br>Am not I [[Hermia]]? Are not you [[Lysander]]?<br>I am as fair now as I was erewhile.<br>Since [[Night\|night]] you loved me, yet since [[Night\|night]] you left me.<br>Why then, you left me—O, the gods forbid—<br>In earnest, shall I say? |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | Ay, by my life,<br>And never did [[Desire\|desire]] to see thee more.<br>Therefore be out of [[Hope\|hope]], of question, [[Doubt\|doubt]].<br>Be certain, nothing truer; ’tis no jest<br>That I do hate thee and [[Love\|love]] [[Helena]]. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | (to [[Helena]])<br>O me, you juggler, you canker blossom,<br>You thief of [[Love\|love]]—what, have you come by [[Night\|night]]<br>And stol’n my [[Love\|love]]’s heart from him? |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | Fine, i’[[Faith\|faith]].<br>Have you no modesty, no maiden shame,<br>No touch of bashfulness? What, will you tear<br>Impatient answers from my gentle tongue?<br>Fie, fie, you counterfeit, you puppet, you! |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | Puppet? Why, so! Ay, that way goes the game.<br>Now I perceive that she hath made compare<br>Between our statures; she hath urged her height,<br>And with her personage, her tall personage,<br>Her height, forsooth, she hath prevailed with him—<br>And are you grown so high in his esteem<br>Because I am so dwarfish and so low?<br>How low am I, thou painted maypole? Speak,<br>How low am I? I am not yet so low<br>But that my nails can reach unto thine eyes. |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | (to [[Demetrius]] and [[Lysander]])<br>I pray you, though you mock me, gentlemen,<br>Let her not hurt me. I was never curst.<br>I have no gift at all in shrewishness.<br>I am a right maid for my cowardice.<br>Let her not strike me. You perhaps may think<br>Because she is something lower than myself<br>That I can match her— |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | Lower? Hark again. |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | Good [[Hermia]], do not be so bitter with me.<br>I evermore did [[Love\|love]] you, [[Hermia]],<br>Did ever keep your counsels, never wronged you—<br>Save that in [[Love\|love]] unto [[Demetrius]]<br>I told him of your stealth unto this wood.<br>He followed you; for [[Love\|love]] I followed him.<br>But he hath chid me hence, and threatened me<br>To strike me, spurn me, nay, to kill me too.<br>And now, so you will let me quiet go,<br>To Athens will I bear my folly back,<br>And follow you no further. Let me go.<br>You see how simple and how fond I am. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | Why, get you gone. Who is’t that hinders you? |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | A foolish heart that I leave here behind. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | What, with [[Lysander]]? |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | With [[Demetrius]]. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | Be not afraid; she shall not harm thee, [[Helena]]. |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | No, sir, she shall not, though you take her part. |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | O, when she is angry she is keen and shrewd.<br>She was a vixen when she went to school,<br>And though she be but little, she is fierce. |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | Little again? Nothing but ‘low’ and ‘little’?—<br>Why will you suffer her to flout me thus?<br>Let me come to her. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | Get you gone, you [[Dwarf\|dwarf]],<br>You minimus of hind’ring knot-grass made,<br>You bead, you acorn. |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | You are too officious<br>In her behalf that scorns your services.<br>Let her [[Alone\|alone]]. Speak not of [[Helena]].<br>Take not her part. For if thou dost intend<br>Never so little show of [[Love\|love]] to her,<br>Thou shalt aby it. |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | Now she holds me not.<br>Now follow, if thou dar’st, to try whose right,<br>Of thine or mine, is most in [[Helena]]. |
| [[Demetrius\|DEMETRIUS]] | Follow? Nay, I’ll go with thee, cheek by jowl. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[Lysander]] and [[Demetrius]] |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | You, mistress, all this coil is long of you.<br>Nay, go not back. |
| [[Helena\|HELENA]] | I will not trust you, I,<br>Nor longer stay in your curst company.<br>Your hands than mine are quicker for a fray;<br>My legs are longer, though, to run away. Exit |
| [[Hermia\|HERMIA]] | I am amazed, and know not what to say. Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Oberon]] and Robin come forward |
| [[Oberon\|OBERON]] | This is thy negligence. Still thou mistak‘st,<br>Or else commit’st thy knaveries wilfully. |
| ROBIN | Believe me, [[King\|king]] of shadows, I mistook.<br>Did not you tell me I should know the man<br>By the Athenian garments he had on?—<br>And so far blameless proves my enterprise<br>That I have ’nointed an Athenian’s eyes;<br>And so far am I glad it so did sort<br>As this their jangling I esteem a sport. |
| [[Oberon\|OBERON]] | Thou seest these [[lovers]] seek a place to fight.<br>Hie therefore, Robin, overcast the [[Night\|night]];<br>The starry welkin cover thou anon<br>With drooping fog as black as Acheron,<br>And lead these testy rivals so astray<br>As one come not within another’s way.<br>Like to [[Lysander]] sometime frame thy tongue,<br>Then stir [[Demetrius]] up with bitter wrong;<br>And sometime rail thou like [[Demetrius]],<br>And from each other look thou lead them thus<br>Till o’er their brows [[Death\|death]]-counterfeiting [[Sleep\|sleep]]<br>With leaden legs and batty wings doth creep.<br>Then crush this herb into [[Lysander]]’s eye—<br>Whose liquor hath this virtuous property,<br>To take from thence all error with his might,<br>And make his eyeballs roll with wonted sight.<br>When they next wake, all this derision<br>Shall seem [[A Dream\|a dream]] and fruitless vision,<br>And back to Athens shall the [[lovers]] wend<br>With league whose date till [[Death\|death]] shall never end.<br>Whiles I in this affair do thee employ,<br>I’ll to my [[Queen\|queen]] and beg her Indian boy;<br>And then I will her charmed eye release<br>From [[Monster\|monster]]’s view, and all things shall be peace. |
| ROBIN | My fairy lord, this must be done with haste,<br>For [[Night\|night]]’s swift dragons cut the clouds full fast,<br>And yonder shines Aurora’s harbinger,<br>At whose approach [[Ghosts\|ghosts]], wand’ring here and there,<br>Troop [[Home\|home]] to churchyards; damned spirits all<br>That in [[Cross\|cross]]-ways and floods have burial<br>Already to their wormy beds are gone,<br>For [[Fear\|fear]] lest day should look their shames upon.<br>They wilfully themselves exiled from light,<br>And must for aye consort with black-browed [[Night\|night]]. |
| [[Oberon\|OBERON]] | But we are spirits of another sort.<br>I with the morning’s [[Love\|love]] have oft made sport,<br>And like a forester the groves may tread<br>Even till the eastern gate, all fiery red,<br>Opening on Neptune with fair blessed beams<br>Turns into yellow gold his salt green streams.<br>But notwithstanding, haste, make no delay;<br>We may effect this business yet ere day. Exit |
| ROBIN | Up and down, up and down,<br>I will lead them up and down.<br>I am feared in field and town.<br>Goblin, lead them up and down.<br>Here comes one. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Lysander]] |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | Where art thou, proud [[Demetrius]]? Speak thou now. |
| *(didascalia)* | ROBIN shifting place |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | Here, [[Villain\|villain]], drawn and ready. Where art thou? |
| [[Lysander\|LYSANDER]] | I will be with thee straight. |
| *(didascalia)* | ROBIN shifting place Follow me then |
| *(didascalia)* | To plainer ground. Exit [[Lysander]] |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Demetrius]] |
